# Worker log

Append-only, written by the worker. One entry per task:

```
## Tn — <title> — done | partial | blocked (<date time>)
Commands: ...
Results: key numbers (link to training/results/*.json)
Commit: <hash>
Deviations / choices: ...
```


Entries validated by the reviewer up to 2026-11-10 04:00 (T0 through RP5, 121 sections) were moved to `old-worker-log.md` (frozen archive, full history). This file keeps the entries still open or awaiting validation.

## FREEZE 1-3 + MOBILE-VOICE + NOISE (2026-11-09)
- **FREEZE 1 (persisted mic, ping-pong).** `settings.handsFree` is migrated to `false` on every load and stripped on save; the auto-start at the end of `app.mjs` is gone. `aborted` (not our own abort) and `audio-capture` now count as failures (backoff, then a visible "Microphone paused"). `createMicLock` in `speech.mjs` (`navigator.locks` + `BroadcastChannel`): a second tab shows "The microphone is in use in another tab — use it here?" and takes over only on the click.
- **FREEZE 2 (hidden tabs).** `pageHidden` / `pageVisible` / `pageHide` on the speech object, wired to `visibilitychange` and `pagehide`: no listening or speaking while hidden, resume only if the mic was on, everything released on pagehide.
- **FREEZE 3 (recognizer churn).** One `SpeechRecognition` object per page (handlers re-assigned per start with a session id, detached when dropped); restarts capped at 30 a minute, then a visible pause.
- **FREEZE 4 coordination.** `speech.mjs` calls `plausible(text, { signal })` with at most one dry run in flight and aborts the older run when a newer text arrives; `app.mjs` passes the signal to `session.dryRead(text, { signal })`. The cancellable `dryRead` itself is the other agent's part.
- **MOBILE-VOICE 1.** After a send the mic pauses until turn, effects and spoken answer are done (`setWorking` from `updateBusy`, "working…" state); no voice queue (a late final leaves its text in the box). **2.** Cumulative finals replace the pending text (`mergeFinal`), one message per utterance, recogniser session restarted after each send; duplicate (edit distance <= 0.2 in 10 s, prefix/suffix), fragment (stop words, one or two words, unless an answer or a known command), rate (1 per 2 s) filters; more than 4 drops in 10 s pause the mic with "I am hearing fragments"; not-understood voice replies never switch the tab or veil the tabs, and repeats within 20 s are collapsed. **3.** Slow devices (`measureSpeed` > 45 ms, or two missed dry-run deadlines): the per-pause dry run is off. Items 4-5 (reply language, diminutives) are not part of this task.
- **NOISE.** `ui/voice-noise.mjs` (`classifyVoice`): ignored voice shows greyed "ignored (not a command)" with "tap to send anyway", phrase log status `ignored`; typed never; questions to the assistant and addressed messages pass; Settings > Voice wake-word checkbox (`wakeWord`).
- **Tests.** `tests/voice-mobile.test.mjs` (18: merge, fragment, duplicate, replacement, rate, restart cap, failures, hidden tab, two-tab lock, slow device, dry-run in flight, noise lines, and the replay of the 108 phone records through a cumulative-final fake recogniser at 1/25 time scale); `scripts/test-webchat-voice.mjs --only mobile,persist,twotabs,noise` (real Chrome; `mobile` also at `--throttle 6`). `tests/speech-endturn.test.mjs` adjusted for the reused recognizer and the new filters (rate and duplicate off there).
- Not done here: the 30-minute soak test and FREEZE 4-5 (other agent); Settings › Models ORT budget.
## FREEZE 4-5 + soak (2026-11-09)
- **Item 4, dry run.** `DialogSession.dryRead(text, { language, signal, deadlineMs, detail })` (src/dialog/index.mjs): honours the caller's `AbortSignal` and its own turn guard at every cancellation point (`dry-translate`, `dry-vocabulary`, `dry-read`, `dry-resolve`), at most one in flight per session (a newer call aborts the older, which answers `reason: 'cancelled'`, and waits for it to unwind), leniency off (it only calls the rules formalizer; no spelling, partial or best effort), rules translator only, skipped for more than 25 words (`too-long`) or when no non-function word is in the spell vocabulary of any world (`no-vocabulary`; with `detail` the skip is explained). The API stays compatible. Measurement (`scripts/measure-dryread.mjs`): mean 48.4 -> 49.5 ms, p95 226 -> 244 ms on 16 noise lines in Node (the reading itself is not shorter); skipped lines 5-24 ms -> 0.5-1.4 ms; the gain is the bound (one at a time, cancellation), not the average. `tests/dry-read.test.mjs`.
- **Item 5, ORT.** `createOrtClient` has a restart budget (`restartBudget`, default 2 per 5 minutes): the third restart pauses the models (worker terminated, no reload, every call fails at once with `OrtPaused`, rules answer), `client.paused`, `resume()`, `stats.pauses`, `onPause`; `measureDeviceSpeed()` (best of three runs of a fixed loop, reference 12 ms, clamped 1..8) is run in `ort-host.mjs` at load and multiplies every deadline and the cancel grace. Settings > Models shows a "Models paused" card with the reason and "Resume models", plus the speed factor and the restart count; a toast on pause. `tests/ort-budget.test.mjs`. Spec 06 updated.
- **Soak.** `scripts/soak-webchat.mjs` (see training/RESULTS.md for the 30-minute numbers on `bf91173`). The 30-minute run: heap 117 -> 138 MB, 4 workers flat, 0 watchdog, 0 ORT restarts, echo 22 ms, busy 56 % average / 75 % worst minute (FAIL), second tab started listening (FAIL). Both failures belong to the parallel speech.mjs rewrite (persisted mic, cross-tab lock) and NOISE (room talk is still full turns); neither had landed on main. Re-run `bash -c` ... `node scripts/soak-webchat.mjs --minutes 30` after they land.
- **Gates (heavy.sh).** `npm test`: 1078 pass, 1 fail (`garbleCandidates "mcdavatara human"`, passes alone; load flake). `npm run test:browser`: 2 fail, both outside this change: the SURPRISE pool "You can call me Ana." (SMALL task) and "a tap on the paused microphone resumes listening" (fails the same way on a clean origin/main checkout).
- Also: `scripts/test-webchat-turns.mjs` now exports `loadPuppeteer`, `findChrome`, `DOCS`, `TYPES`.

## R5b (2026-11-09)
Result: NOT PUBLISHED. Four segmentation-focused v5 retrains (12 checkpoints) all stay below v4 on the long set; v4e + N1 v3 stays published. Only the option and the logs are committed.
**Option:** `--seg-long-extra N` in `psys2_train.dispatcher`: N extra long messages (14-40 routing sentences, family long) generated anew every segmentation pass. Runs (R5 setting, distilbert, lr 5e-5, 6 epochs, W 2.0, 3,918-3,924 steps, 4 in parallel, about 2.4 h): a seed 1 `--seg-extra-n 500`; b seed 2 `--seg-extra-n 1000`; c seed 3 `--seg-extra-n 1000 --seg-long-extra 1500`; d seed 4 `--seg-extra-n 500 --seg-long-extra 1500`. Test world top-1 .9728 / .9734 / .9717 / .9699. (Excluding the diagram segments from the long passes is not a separate case: they are extra short multi-clause messages appended to the pass, never mixed into long ones; n=500/1000 vs 6000 covers that.) Diagram test split top-1/top-3 not re-measured because no candidate reaches publish.
**Bucket eval** (`eval_buckets`, current test multicmd; final correct (omitted, duplicates)), v4e and v5s2 measured here:
| run-ckpt | 2-3 | 6-8 | long |
| v4e-2448 (published) | .643 (21, 839) | .254 (14, 373) | .090 (58, 2041) |
| v5s2-3265 (R5 best) | .743 (37, 211) | .299 (34, 83) | .095 (142, 437) |
| a-3924 | .738 (40, 179) | .302 (33, 77) | .090 (156, 399) |
| b-3265 | .764 (43, 180) | .347 (33, 82) | .117 (170, 403) |
| b-3918 | .755 (38, 186) | .345 (32, 72) | .110 (156, 395) |
| c-3265 | .758 (35, 208) | .322 (33, 89) | .105 (139, 452) |
| c-3918 | .750 (38, 214) | .314 (33, 91) | .098 (136, 461) |
| d-3265 | .761 (43, 182) | .345 (32, 82) | .090 (159, 448) |
| d-3918 | .762 (43, 176) | .350 (34, 80) | .095 (149, 437) |
(All 12 in `training/results/r5b-*-test-buckets.json`.) Finding: v5 removes most duplicates (2041 -> 400-460) but has 2.5-3x the long-bucket omissions of v4e (136-170 vs 58) in every run, whatever the diagram segment count or the extra long messages; lowering `--seg-extra-n` to 500 and adding 1500 long messages per pass does not move omissions below 136. The omissions come from the new data mix (avatar/editor/diagram phrasing), not from the amount of diagram segments.
**Operational long set (154, encoder+n1), measured on the tree with the validator of 205e28b (see the tree finding below):** v4e 94.8 right / 1.3 wrong ([long] 91.7 / 0); v5s2-3265 93.5 / 3.2 (86.1 / 5.6); a-3924 91.6 / 4.5; b-3265 91.6 / 3.9; b-3918 90.9 / 4.5; c-3265 90.9 / 5.2 (77.8 / 11.1); c-3918 92.2 / 4.5; d-3265 91.6 / 4.5; d-3918 92.2 / 3.9. None reaches 94.8. Main 1296, dialogues, Romanian and eval-diagram were not run (no candidate passes the first gate).
**Finding about the tree (important, not caused by any model):** current main gives v4e + N1 only **80.5 right / 13.0 wrong** on the long set (published model, `--mode encoder+n1`; [long] 61.1 / wrong 22.2), against 94.8 / 1.3 at 205e28b and 51a43f1. Bisected: first bad commit is 10f9fd7 (DIAG2). Reverting only `src/dialog/` of that commit (dispatch-validate.mjs + index.mjs, the new `tabTier` policy / ADR-004 tier T) restores 94.8 / 1.3; reverting `worlds/diagram.sketch` alone does not. Tier T accepts a long message as ONE clause of the active tab's world when the tab's rules read it completely (`res.segments.length === 1`), so a long multi-command message that one tab pattern swallows is no longer segmented by the dispatcher. The main set was not re-measured; the long set (and probably multi-command messages in general) lost 14 points, so the DIAG2 owner needs to restrict tier T (for example: not when the dispatcher proposes more than one segment, or only for short messages) before any dispatcher publish is judged. Candidate evaluations above therefore ran in a scratch worktree with `src/dialog` from 10f9fd7~1 (identical to 205e28b behaviour) and are comparable with v4.
**Decision:** fail (long right 90.9-93.5 < 94.8, long wrong +2.6 to +3.9 pt over v4); v4 stays. Not done: dialogues / Romanian / eval-diagram (no candidate passes).

## TIER-T-LONG (2026-11-09)
Result: fixed. Tier T no longer hides the dispatcher's segmentation of long multi-command messages. Change in `src/dialog/dispatch-validate.mjs` (`tabFirst`, new policy field `tabTierLongWords` = 8), ADR-004 amendment, spec 03 section 7.13a.
- **Rule.** If the dispatcher's best segmentation has more than one piece, the message has more than 8 words and it is not running text (no sentence end followed by more text), the tab reading needs a `signal=true` pattern in every clause (the multi-clause condition of SMALL 4). Diagram messages are unaffected (their patterns carry the signal).
- **Tried and rejected:** (a) skip tier T when the pieces are of different worlds: long set 91.6 right / 4.5 wrong (same-world multi-commands in art.draw and data.charts still swallowed); (b) skip on any dispatcher split without the length / running-text conditions: long set 94.2 / 1.9 but the 1296 set got 4 more wrong (docs.editor "delete the last word", "delete the last sentence", dictated sentences "write It was cold. The fox ...").
- **Before / after** (published v4e + N1, `--mode encoder+n1`, `--jobs 10`, `--no-write`):

| measure | before (main) | after |
| --- | --- | --- |
| long set (154) right / partly / wrong | 80.5 / 3.2 / 13.0 | 94.2 / 3.2 / 1.9 (mid 94.9 / wrong 2.5, long 91.7 / wrong 0) |
| 1296 operational right / wrong | 98.4 / 0.6 (8 wrong; the 98.5 / 0.3 baseline measured at SMALL 3/4) | 98.6 / 0.3 (1 known_gap chess#039 changes to not understood) |
| eval-diagram `--set test`, wire-level act right | 81.0 (the rules router, tier T is not involved) | 81.0 |

The target 94.8 / 1.3 was measured on an earlier tree; the same published models on the pre-DIAG2 validator today give the 94.2 / 1.9 reported at line "edit head off" above, so 94.2 / 1.9 is the like-for-like number.
- **Tests.** `test-world diagram.sketch` 756/756; tests/dispatch-validate + tests/diagram* 51 pass; browser test `scripts/test-webchat-diagram3.mjs` 15/15 rows pass (webchat rebuilt). `npm test` 1111 tests, 1110 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped.
## MOBILE-VOICE / FREEZE 1-3 / NOISE review fixes at 6x (2026-11-09)
- **Two tabs.** The old owner now also learns it lost the microphone when its lock is stolen (the `navigator.locks` request rejects), not only from the late channel message; `twotabs` asserts the first tab stops within 2 s and passes at `--throttle 6`.
- **Noise without the dry run.** `classifyVoice` falls back to `cheap()` (command verb or domain word near the start, question word first, one-word texts are room talk) whenever the dry run is off (slow device), busy, late or inconclusive. At `--throttle 6` `noise` passes: 4 greyed + 4 dropped fragments of 8, 0 replies, 4 `ignored` records, "tap to send anyway" works, real commands run. Unit tests cover the cheap path for the 8 room-talk lines and 7 commands.
- **Browser replay.** `scripts/test-webchat-voice.mjs --only replay --throttle 6` feeds the 108 phone records through the cumulative-final fake recogniser with real gaps (cut at 6 s): 64 heard, 44 arrived while the mic was paused, 15 messages for 56 utterance gaps, queue 0, mic never live during a turn, no fragment sent, one tab switch (the real word "desene", which opens the draw tab), heart and house in the state, 0 page errors.
- **Soak findings (3 min, 4x, encoder+normalizer).** The first run paused the mic at t=60 s ("too many restarts": every message caused two planned restarts). Now only idle restarts (a session that ended without a result) count toward the 30 a minute cap, and a rate-guard drop no longer counts toward the fragment pause. Result: 10/10, main-thread busy 26% average (worst minute 29%, was 56% before NOISE), 0 ORT restarts, the second tab never started listening and the first kept listening (25 to 43 messages).

## NX2 pilot (agent B, 2026-11-10)

- Gold built with `node scripts/build-nx-gold.mjs` then `node scripts/build-nx-real50.mjs` (real labels = Sonnet + hand check, 9 corrections), 300 items over 21 worlds.
- Prepared with `node scripts/nx-pilot.mjs prepare` (22 batches, 3 shards of 100 items); labelers: subagents `sonnet` and `haiku` over each shard (one session per shard file), scored with `node scripts/nx-pilot.mjs score <labeler>`; vLLM: `training/.venv-vllm/bin/python training/nx/vllm_label.py --model Qwen/Qwen3-30B-A3B-Instruct-2507-FP8 --name qwen30 --batches .../pilot/batches-all.json --out .../pilot/out/qwen30` (needs `PATH` with the venv bin and `C_INCLUDE_PATH` with the Python headers, see RESULTS.md).
- Judges: `node scripts/nx-pilot.mjs judge-prepare sonnet haiku`, two subagent sessions per judge, `judge-score`. Bug found and fixed on the way: two judge calls shared the id `ui.avatar.0` (second call renamed `ui.avatar.1.c0`, its two verdicts taken from the judges' reports).
- The Sonnet judge wrote most verdict files through a small Python script (the verdicts are its own); not repeated in later runs.
- Result: labeler Sonnet (95.0 / 0.922), judge Sonnet (no judge qualifies). 70-120B model skipped for lack of disk (49 GB free).

## RP5b: what still failed after cc78918 (Romanian voice session, real Chrome, default settings)

- **Root cause of rp5-18/19.** The tab strip never told the engine which tab the user opened: a click only called `showTab`, so `ui.shell.activeTab` stayed on the old tab and tier T (spec 03) read "desenează trei cercuri" with the wrong tab (it ran in node because the test harness sets the tab by `setWorldState`). `docs/webchat/app.mjs` now has `userTab` (click and arrow keys set `activeTab` in the session). Second part: "in the diagram" on the Drawing tab was read as a place by `art.draw` (the tab's catch-all pattern beat the diagram match); new `pat.diagram.sketch.create.in` (`signal=true`) makes "in/on/to the diagram" name the world, in English and Romanian. `diagramă` translates to "diagram" (lexicon), `grafic` stays "chart".
- **Tab names.** Frames `deschide|arată|afișează|mergi la|treci pe ... tabul|fila|... <name>` (and the bare form) for desen, desenat, diagramă, grafic(e), hartă, muzică, editor, șah, spânzurătoarea, x și zero, ghicește animalul, documente, avatar, profil, plus a generic `{np}` frame; replies "Bine, iată fila Desen." / "Ești deja pe fila Avatar." (no more "Am înțeles asta: open the ..." because a frame reads the sentence now).
- **Replies in Romanian.** New `src/translate/avatar.en-ro.mjs` (authored Romanian for every variant of the robot, emoji, person, cartoon and kid parts; slots `{avl}`, `{chr}`, option lists per part), the mouth-size refusal for every part, tic-tac-toe start, missing-place prompt with examples, chart "no numbers" help, "Drew c1, c2 and c3.", "at the bottom". The translator's leftover check ignores quoted text (it made "OK, I added the heading "Shopping"." stay English).
- **Wording.** "mută peștele în apă" says "Am mutat peștele în apă." and adds the water when there is none ("Nu era apă, așa că am adăugat-o pe pământ."); "o gură grătar" (the `{avl}` entries come before the generic noun-phrase ones).
- **check-ro-replies** now lists the English-kept replies by family (shape and count): 377 -> 346 kept in English over 2480 cases at the end of this task, 145 families, the largest still English are the world-help sentences with quoted examples ("I do not know what "..." is", the Avatar tab help, "Did you mean ..."), the Romania borders / distance / key facts, the chess intro. They are not part of what the user hit today.
- **Left.** rp5-21 (background talk) stays with NOISE / MOBILE-VOICE; the voice files (`speech.mjs`, `voice-noise.mjs`, the voice parts of `app.mjs`) were not touched, only the tab functions of `app.mjs`. The describe family of `ui.avatar` (`_describe`, "I am Name, a robot. ... My body is ...") and the portrait / pixel characters have no Romanian phrases yet, and "Fă o gură mare" still answers with the style refusal (now in Romanian) instead of a bigger smile.
- **Tests.** tests/ro-rp5.test.mjs 13 pass (5 new); `scripts/test-webchat-ro-rp5.mjs` (new, default settings, `language: 'ro'`, 20 rows of expectations-2026-11-09e except rp5-21 plus every tab name and the two wording rows) all rows pass; `test-world` diagram.sketch 756/756, art.draw 560/560, ui.shell 64/64.

## NX (Track 2, agent A, second shift): full NX5, NX4-C, browser cost, NX3 retrain (2026-11-10)
Result: the full pipeline evaluation exists for rules, A, A+, A3, A+3 in four configurations; the numbers and the recommendation are in `training/RESULTS.md` section NX (NX5, NX3 data in the models, NX4-C, browser cost, recommendation). Short version: the model alone reads 62-66 % of the operational set (rules 95.6 %), D (rules first, model only for segments without a complete reading, validator always) is neutral on the operational set (+2 of 1,450), clearly better on paraphrases outside the patterns (gold-para 22 -> 56-63 %), and the model reading alone is the better reader there (71-82 %). Nothing in `src/`, `worlds/` or `docs/webchat` was changed.
- **Pipeline.** `scripts/lib/nx-formalizer.mjs` (strategy built outside `src/`: op + BIO spans -> synthesized sentence from the candidate pattern -> rules' own canonicalisation and emit -> legend check -> interpreter dry run on a session fork), `scripts/lib/nx-runtime.mjs` (systems), `scripts/eval-nx.mjs` (one system, all sets, `--jobs 16`), `scripts/nx5-report.mjs`, `tests/nx-formalizer.test.mjs` (4 pass). Every system: `bash heavy.sh node scripts/eval-nx.mjs --system <name> --ort <ort> --jobs 16` (35-45 s each).
- **Two bugs found on the way, both visible in the numbers.** (1) Patterns of one operation that differ only in a constant (`where.op=plus` against `minus`) cannot be told apart by the heads, so the candidate patterns are ranked by how many of their own frame words the segment contains (kids.arith 18 -> 30 right of the first 40). (2) The first version of D let the model read before the engine's spelling leniency and lost 16 correct operational cases to confident wrong readings of typos ("mke the mouth grill"); D now wraps the session's `_formalize` at run time (rules + the engine's leniency first, then the model for what is still not complete). The validator also needed a fork that holds the wires of the earlier segments of the message.
- **NX3.** Agent B's `training/dataset/nx/final` (13,082 items; 17 removed because their text equals a protected text: `final-filtered/`). A and A+ retrained on NX1 + NX3 (`nx-a-nx3`, `nx-aplus-nx3`, exports `training/runs/export/nx-a-nx3`, `nx-aplus-nx3`); `nx_heads.py --data` takes comma-separated globs now. The data helps the paraphrase sets and costs about 1-2 points on the operational set.
- **NX4-C.** `training/psys2_train/nx_lora.py`, LoRA r=8 on 6 layers x 6 targets and the two heads, per world; adapters `training/runs/export/nx-c/<world>.onnx_adapter` (2.75 MB each) on a 70.6 MB base graph; checked in onnxruntime-web (1x and 4x). Gotchas: all-zero B matrices are merged by the ONNX exporter (1e-7 noise per matrix), the exported initializer names carry the wrapper's `m.` prefix, `quantize_dynamic` needs the LoRA matmuls in `nodes_to_exclude`.
- **Browser cost.** `training/experiments/browser-limits/nx-run.mjs`: model A 68.0 MB (+0.9 MB), 50 ms for 32 tokens at 1x and 172 ms at 4x, the same as dispatcher v4.
- **Not done.** NX5 end to end with the LoRA adapters; B in the browser (not worth it: 40 passes); the confidence gate for D. Per-item results `training/results/nx5/*.json` are not committed (0.9 MB each, regenerated in about 40 s per system).
- **Cleanup.** Nothing in `training/runs` was deleted (a symlink `training/runs` -> the main checkout's directory exists in the worktree and is ignored by `.gitignore` only as a directory: never `git add -A` there).
## SOAK-BUSY (FREEZE-6, 2026-11-10)
Goal: soak main-thread busy < 50% average and worst minute low, without loosening the gate. Harness additions in `scripts/soak-webchat.mjs`: `--profile [--profile-out f]` (CDP Profiler for the whole run: self and inclusive top lists, idle vs `(program)` vs JS vs GC), `--trace f` (Chrome tracing, devtools.timeline), and a per-turn summary from `__psys2Perf` (stages, render, effects).
**Where the time went (4x, 3-minute profile, before):** idle 43%, native `(program)` 36% (rendering), JS 18%, GC 3%. Trace of the main thread over 126 s: 24 s microtask JS, and about 19 s of Layout / UpdateLayoutTree / PrePaint / Paint / Layerize (about 55 frames per second of paint all the time, because the listening mic button animated `box-shadow`, a non-compositor property, in an infinite loop; 14,700 paints).
Top 3 before: (1) page repaints every frame (mic pulse): about 15% of wall; (2) rules formalization (`formalize` inclusive 8% of wall; callers: leniency `parse` 5.7%, validator `check` 2.5%, dry runs 3.3%); (3) dry run + NOISE classifier reading the same text twice (`_dryRun` 3.3%, `classifyVoice` 1.7%) and forced layouts (`getBoundingClientRect` / `scrollDown`).
**Changes.** (a) `style.css`: the mic and record pulses animate `transform` and `opacity` of a pseudo-element (compositor only); Paint 14.7k -> 7.9k events, layout-tree updates 6.9k -> 5.0k in the same 2-minute trace. (b) `DialogSession.dryRead`: a settled answer is reused for 5 s for the same text, language, tab and session state (`DRY_CACHE_MS`, `_rev` bumped by `setWorldState` / `observe`); cancellation, deadline, busy and error answers are never cached; spec 06 updated. (c) `correctTokens`: the closest-vocabulary-word search is memoized per vocabulary object and word (it scanned the whole vocabulary for every unknown word of every reading). (d) `app.mjs`: `label()`, status line, send button (icon rebuilt only when it changes) and mic aria state write the DOM only on a change; `scrollDown` once per animation frame; the ring restart no longer forces a layout.
**Numbers** (host load varies a lot: the same code gave 27%, 33% and 54% in three 3-minute runs, startup dominates short runs):
| run | before | after |
| 3 min, 4x, `base3` | 57% avg / 72% worst minute | |
| 3 min, 6x | 42% / 57% (p6) | 33% / 48%, 27% / 34%, 54% / 73% (variance) |
| 10 min, 4x, final | | 34% avg / 57% worst minute, 130 messages (the 30-minute baseline was 52% / 91%) |
Remaining top costs after: rules `formalize` 11% of wall (leniency `parse` 6%, validator `check` 2.7%, `correctTokens` 2.5%), GC 3%, remaining layout about 7%. Average gate passes; the worst minute (57%) is still above the harness 50% gate and is below 70%. Turns with encoder + validation cost about 1-2 s of main thread each at 4x; a worker for `DialogSession` was not started (formalization stays under 300 ms per rules read).
**Tests.** `npm test`: 1126 tests, 1125 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped. `npm run test:browser`: 3 of 4 files pass; the voice file hit its 300 s timeout under the shared lock; run alone `scripts/test-webchat-voice.mjs` all 101 checks PASS, 0 FAIL.

## GAPS-1b (worker, 2026-11-10)
- **1. Complete pattern reads that swallow a long input.** Root cause: `pat.art.draw.add` (`I want` is a draw lead, `ball` a drawable) with `{w:rest}` took "and a pencil and pay with 20 lei. what's the change"; the kids.shop pattern `q.change.want.c` (priority 11) already matched but lost to priority 12. The gluing in `normalize.mjs` and the phrases already handled "what's" and the missing period, so no change there. New `applyCoveragePolicy` / `PATTERN_COVERAGE_POLICY = { minWords: 8, minCoverage: 0.35, betterCoverage: 0.6, dropWhenAlone: false }` in `src/formalizer/rules/index.mjs`, applied to the matches of a segment before routing: a match whose fixed part (tokens outside `words`/`rest` slots) covers < 35% of a segment of 8+ tokens is dropped when a match of ANOTHER world covers >= 60%. First try with a competitor threshold of 35% broke `Move the balloon to the bottom left corner` (art.draw.move vs diagram.sketch.move.at at 0.38), hence `betterCoverage`.
- **"Nothing else reads it" case** (`dropWhenAlone: true`, tried with 8/0.35, 10/0.25): operational right 1239 -> 1230 (wrong 6 -> 11), long set right 87 -> 100 (wrong 35 -> 21), dialogues 97% -> 95.8% turns; it breaks docs.editor free text ("write It was cold. ...") and a few chart commands, helps long multi-command messages in music/charts. Left off; the option is in the policy for a later, finer rule (e.g. only `rest` slots that contain a clause boundary).
- **2. Bare names.** The "Which Move" read came from `pat.kids.common.w.meet.f7` ("{n:words(3)} is best") feeding `chat.meet`, not from user.profile. `chat.meet` now refuses question words, auxiliaries, articles, pronouns and common words (`NOT_A_NAME`), with a "Sorry, I did not understand that." reply for question-shaped names; `user.profile` `properName` refuses text ending in `?` and wh-word / auxiliary starts. "Zed is fine by me" still gives "Nice to meet you, Zed!".
- Tests/data: `tests/pattern-coverage.test.mjs` (5), kids.shop tests gaps1b1/2, 3 dialogues `dlg.gaps1b#*` (family real, tag gaps), spec 03 section 7.15 updated, webchat rebuilt. (A test-world case for the name refusal is not possible: the default test tab answers "which move is best" with a tic-tac-toe hint; the unit test and the dialogue use the hangman tab.)
- **Numbers (rules mode, before -> after).** Operational n 1296: right 1239 -> 1239, partly 22 -> 22, wrong 6 -> 6, not understood 29 -> 29. Long set n 154: right 87 -> 87, partly 14 -> 14, wrong 35 -> 35, not understood 18 -> 18. Dialogues 461 -> 464 (3 new, all right), turns 2213 -> 2222, 90.0% -> 90.1% dialogues right, 97% turns. `npm test`: 1163 tests, 1161 pass, 0 fail, 2 skipped. test-world: kids.shop 147/147, kids.common 363/363, user.profile 124/124, art.draw 560/560.

## ELLIPSIS: "and a yellow one" repeats the previous command (from the URGENT-FREEZE side note)
- Bug: "fă o casă verde și una galbenă" drew only the green house. Two causes: the English "draw a green house and a yellow one" was read whole by `art.draw.add` with `{w:rest}` swallowing "and a yellow one"; and the translator rendered "una mare" as "a sea" and "încă una / alta galbenă" without "one".
- Translator (`src/translate/phrases.ro-en.mjs`): frames `una|unul {adj}` -> "a {adj} one", `încă|alta|altul ... {col|adj}` -> "another {adj} one" (spec 05).
- Formalizer (`oneAnaphora` in `src/formalizer/rules/expand.mjs`, hook in `expandClauses`, `swallowsAnaphora` in `isWeak` of `rules/index.mjs`): an elliptic clause `[a|an|another|N]? mods one|ones` / `one mods` repeats the previous full clause with the new modifiers (any world; verb + determiner of the earlier clause, noun singularized when needed, earlier modifiers of another lexicon class kept: "big green house and a yellow one" -> big yellow house; the class of a word is the smallest lexicon holding it). Accepted only if the same world reads it (usual guard). Phrasing: `lang.elide.lead` / `lang.elide.noun` in `worlds/lang.common/lexicon.sop`. `matcher.mjs`: tokens may carry `textOverride`. Spec 03 section 1c "One-anaphora".
- Tests: `tests/ellipsis-one.test.mjs` (translator, oneAnaphora unit, Romanian and English end to end), 2 art.draw world tests (562 -> 564 would be shown by test-world art.draw), 2 dialogues family real/ellipsis (`ellipsis1`, 6 turns, rules mode 100 %).
- Rules-mode numbers before -> after: operational 1239/1296 right, 6 wrong (same); long 87/154, 35 wrong (same); operational-ro 2035/2480, 61 wrong (same); dialogues 90.1 % dialogues right on 464 -> 466 (the 2 new right).
- Not covered: hybrid/model path (the segmenter's piece "a yellow one" is retried alone, without the anchor) was not changed or measured here; webchat rebuilt.
- ELLIPSIS hybrid check (encoder+n1, the webchat default, ORT for Node): the dispatcher path already gives both objects, no extra code needed. Probe with default settings on the Drawing and Avatar tabs: "fă o casă verde și una galbenă" -> house:green + house:yellow; "desenează un cerc roșu și unul albastru", "desenează două pisici și una mare"; English "draw a green house and a yellow one", "add a red circle and a blue one", "draw two cats and a big one", "draw a house and another yellow one": all correct. The two ellipsis1 dialogues are 100 % in encoder+n1 too. encoder+n1 before -> after: operational 1278/1296 right, 4 wrong (same); long 145/154, 3 wrong (same); operational-ro 2127/2480, 64 wrong (same); dialogues 89.4 % -> 89.5 % of dialogues right (466 vs 464, 2 new right), turns 96.8 % both. Replaces the "Not covered" note above.
## URGENT-FREEZE (2026-11-10): the user's real Chrome freezes on every command — not reproduced; cache-consistency hardening landed
- Tried to reproduce, five ways, none froze: (1) `scripts/freeze-profile.mjs` (new): a Chrome profile populated by the previous build (commit 1e667bb: old model sha 6c41eb24 in the Cache API `psys2-dispatcher-model`, HTTP cache, settings, phrase log), then the same origin switched to the current build, served like Pages (max-age=600, ETag, 304), with index.html revalidated (the ten minutes have passed) but every other file still fresh in the HTTP cache; 4x CPU throttle; typed "make a house and a man", "fă o casă verde și una galbenă", "what is 12 plus 30", "desenează trei cercuri", then voice (fake always-on recogniser, interim words + final) of three commands. Result: typed 105-990 ms, voice 790-1710 ms, key echo 18-27 ms, ORT restarts 0, paused false, 0 page errors, longest main-thread task 774-796 ms (page start, once), no chain of long tasks. (2) The same against the real Pages URL (https://ml-circuits-research.github.io/psys2/webchat/, unthrottled): typed 53-272 ms, voice 0.8-1.2 s, longest task 179 ms, 0 restarts. (3) Old profile -> current main with the cached old modules: the old HTML/JS combination runs fine on the new model (the old worker ignores `fetch`, the old runtime ignores the extra outputs).
- CPU profile of the new-build phase (80 s, 4x): main thread 93 % idle; the busiest functions are the rules matcher (about 0.4 s per 4 turns), `cache.put` and `sha256Hex` of the two models at load (0.2-0.4 s once), GC. The ORT work is in the worker. ORT stats: 24 requests, 24 responses, 0 timeouts, 0 restarts.
- Honest conclusion: the cause on the user's machine is NOT found. What the code review did show: GitHub Pages sends max-age=600 for everything, so for ten minutes after each deploy a browser mixes old and new modules (the module graph, `ui/ort-worker.mjs` and `worlds/index.json` had unversioned URLs), and a persisted model entry was keyed by sha only. Both are fixed below as hardening. Missing `op_logits`/`tag_logits` already degrade to rules (`passOn` returns null, counted as `nx.no_heads`, no loop), the ORT client has a restart budget (2 per 5 min, then the models pause), and the guard bounds the NX reading; I found no unbounded main-thread loop. If it still freezes after this deploy, the next data needed is a performance trace from the user's Chrome (DevTools > Performance, 10 s while typing a command) and `__psys2Ort.stats` plus `caches.keys()` from the console.
- Fixes: (3) cache busting: `scripts/build-webchat.mjs` writes an import map into index.html that sends every module URL (app, ui, lib, world ops, not vendor) to `?v=<content hash>`, preloads the same URLs, and exposes `window.__psys2Mod` (hashes); the ORT worker URL (`ui/ort-host.mjs`) and `worlds/index.json` (early fetch and app fallback) take their version from it, so one index.html always names one consistent set of files and unchanged files stay cached across deploys. The dispatcher model cache key now carries the sha and the output names (`&out=seg+world+edit+op+tag`), a cached model that does not match is never read, and stale entries are evicted on a cache hit too (`ui/dispatcher-host.mjs`). (2) `scripts/soak-webchat.mjs --typed` types the real messages (profile, trace and busy checks cover them); the turn itself needed no extra bound (largest turn-time task 150 ms at 4x). (4) `tests/webchat-freeze.test.mjs`: old-build-populated profile (git archive of 1e667bb when present, plus planted stale model entries) -> current build: every command answered, echo < 100 ms, no two tasks > 500 ms back to back, no chain over 4 s, 0 restarts, 0 errors, every module URL versioned, only the current model in the cache.
- Observed while testing, not a bug of this task: a voice utterance sent within 500 ms after a bot reply is dropped (the recogniser is paused while the bot speaks); the harness waits 4 s between spoken commands.

## COMPUTE-BUDGET C (2026-11-10, worker): per-turn cost (item 7) and wrong answers (item 8)
- Profile (Node `--cpu-prof` on a rules-mode `DialogSession` over all worlds, translator on; plus headless Chrome 4x CPU profile of the built webchat): the four heavy turns are not heavy in steady state here (Node: house 55 ms, kids 40, colour 30, sun 8; browser 4x engine time 227 / 125 / 104 / 76 ms). The reviewer's 4-6 s is not reproduced. Top self time, all four: `matcher.run` (about 25 % of the turn, 1,000-3,200 `matchTokens` calls per turn because clause splitting, one-anaphora expansion, leniency, spelling and partial windows re-read the same sentence), `prefilterPasses` over every pattern for every view, GC, `oneAnaphoraEnv` (rebuilt from all lexicons on every formalize call), spelling (`spellAlternates`, `editDistance`, `spellCorrect`), and on the FIRST turn `worldVocabulary` / `vocabularyOf` per world (lazy, 40-90 ms) and the pattern prefilter sets.
- Fixes: (1) `patternIndex` in `rules/index.mjs`: inverted index of the compiled patterns by their rarest mandatory-literal set, built once per world set, candidates in pattern order (same result as the prefilter on every pattern); (2) `matchTokens` memoized per formalize call on (pattern, token view) when the match did not touch the dialogue state (`env.touched`), 3,240 calls -> 1,688 real matches on the house turn; (3) `oneAnaphoraEnv` memoized per world set; (4) `engine.precompile` also builds the pattern index and the global and per-world spelling vocabularies, in slices, so the first message does not (first turn after precompile in Node: colour 159 -> 76 ms, house 174 -> 53, sun 18 -> 9). Readings are unchanged (all evals below identical).
- Per-turn engine time, steady state in Node (min of 25 runs, base -> now): house "fă o casă verde și una galbenă" 55 -> 27 ms, "I have two kids" 32 -> 10 (now a profile fact, see below), "my favourite color is green" 29 -> 23, "desenează un soare" 9 -> 7. Headless Chrome 4x `engineMs` of the page (last of three runs; first run 906 -> 684 house, 535 -> 168 kids): house 227 -> 132, kids 125 -> 60, colour 104 -> 126 (noise: the page is the same for it), sun 76 -> 50. The 4x goal is NOT reached except for the kids turn; the remaining cost of an unparsed turn is the number of re-reads (about 1,000 matches for "my favourite color is green"), which needs a change of the pipeline (read each window once) and was left alone.
- Item 8 (all rules + encoder+n1): (a) "I like chess, music and maps" opened two tabs: the dispatcher or the clause splitter cut it into "I like chess", "music", "maps". New `lang.self.like` phrase (lang.common) and pattern `pat.user.profile.likes` (`{l:words(8,noun)}`, priority 8, `whole=true`): a preference statement is remembered as hobbies (every item of the list, lexicon-classified when known, one-letter typos of one-word entries repaired: "hikng" is hiking), never opens tabs or draws. New slot guard `words(N,noun)` (refuses an infinitive, "I like to draw a cat" stays a drawing, and a `very` / `much` lead from a garbled translation, which had made two operational-ro cases wrong). New pattern key `whole=true` and `wholeFirst` in `dispatch-validate.mjs`: when the dispatcher cuts a message into pieces but the rules read the whole as one segment with such a pattern, that reading stands. (b) "I have two kids" drew 2 blue people: new `children` field (kb + PLAN_FALLBACK, section basics, `askable=false`) and patterns `children` / `children.one` (phrases `lang.self.have`, `lang.child.noun`). (c) "I am 45 years old" was refused as sensitive: age is now a plain, never-asked field `age` (patterns `sens.age` -> `prof.tell kind=age`, `age.is` "my age is 45", phrases `lang.self.age`, `lang.self.age.is`); "remember that I am 34" still makes a private note; the old dialogue `dlg.g7#023` and tests t030 / profile.test / datagen were changed to the new rule (spec 12 updated, the plan is now 23 fields: "(n of 23 known)"). (d) "make your eyes blue" on the Drawing tab recoloured the suns: `youTarget` in `rankMatches` drops other worlds' readings of a "your ..." target when ui.avatar reads the sentence too (coordinations like "your eyes and body" stay with the expansion), and the dispatcher candidate check refuses such a candidate; spec 03 updated.

Rules: operational n 1296 right 1239 -> 1239, wrong 6 -> 6; long n 154 right 87 -> 87, wrong 35 -> 35; operational-ro n 2480 right 2035 -> 2035, wrong 61 -> 61 (it was 65 until the `very` guard); dialogues 466 -> 470 (420 -> 424 right, the only changed failure texts are the "of 21/23 known" counters). encoder+n1: operational 1278 right, 4 wrong -> same; long 145 right, 3 wrong -> same; operational-ro 2127 right, 64 wrong -> same; dialogues 417/466 -> 421/470 (the 4 new ones right, no regression). Latency p50/p95 of the operational set in rules mode (20 shards, noisy machine): 52 / 403 ms -> 31 / 218 ms. ## COMPUTE-BUDGET B (items 2 and 4): step budgets at every loop site, static loop guard (2026-11-10)
- Built: `src/dialog/budget.mjs` (`createBudget`, `BudgetExceeded {site, ticks, limit, bySite, stack_}`, shared handle `budget.tick(site, n)`, `installBudget/releaseBudget`, `UNLIMITED`), `src/dialog/budget-limits.mjs` (per-site and per-turn limits), `DialogSession.send(text, { budget })` and `dryRead` install a budget per turn, operations get `ctx.budget`. Out of budget: session restored, reply `tooComplex: true` with text "That was too complex for me — try a shorter sentence.", `reply.budget` (ticks, top sites, exceeded) and `reply.budgetExceeded` = { site, ticks, limit, bySite (full table), input, tab, stage, stack (short caller id) }, also in `trace.budgetExceeded`; `dryRead` answers `reason: 'budget'` with the same record. `docs/webchat/ui/phrase-log.mjs` `buildRecord` carries `budget` and `budgetExceeded` into the phrase-log record (agent A: read `reply.budgetExceeded` / `err.name === 'BudgetExceeded'` for no-repeat).
- Sites with ticks: matcher.attempt/backtrack, rules.pattern/readgroup/sentence, normalize.fixpoint, spell.edit (every edit-distance call), dialog.*, discourse.*, interpreter.visit, ops.call, translate.* (tokenize, parse, clause, frame, np, render, items, segment), soplang.*, wordpiece.*, voice.rec, dispatch.segmentation/rewrite, normalizer.decode. Tools to re-measure: `node scripts/measure-ticks.mjs [--mode encoder+n1 --ort ...]`, `node scripts/why-budget.mjs` (turns of a set that trip the budget), `node scripts/loop-violations.mjs`.
- Loop guard: `tests/loop-guard.test.mjs` + `scripts/lib/loop-scan.mjs` (src, worlds/*/ops.mjs, docs/webchat/ui; lib is generated). Violations before: 145 (while/do/for(;;)/custom for/worklist for-of/self-recursion). After: 0. Real ticks where termination is data-dependent (matcher, translator drivers, soplang, wordpiece, normalizer decode, fixpoints); the rest carry a `// bounded: <reason>` comment (index only moves forward over a finite list, list shrinks, Euclid, seen-set, 9-ply game tree ...). Not ticked: helpers of world operations that have no `ctx` (diagram placeFree grid, art.draw placement): constant-size bounded comments; the turn watchdog is the backstop there.
- First limits (10x p99 of rules mode) tripped 10 long encoder+n1 messages (interpreter.visit max 443 k) and one perTurn; limits are now max(10x p99, 2x max) per site, measured in both modes. Table (encoder+n1, 7484 turns, ticks per turn): rules.pattern p99 425 k max 4.0 M (limit 8 M); matcher.backtrack 138 k / 815 k (2 M); spell.edit 89 k / 191 k (1 M); matcher.attempt 29 k / 187 k (400 k); interpreter.visit 25 k / 443 k (1 M); translate.frame 14 k / 26 k (200 k); soplang.entry 3.1 k / 92 k (200 k); soplang.wire 579 / 15.7 k (40 k); translate.tokenize 1.1 k / 2.0 k; rules.readgroup 510 / 8.4 k; wordpiece.end 249 / 433; normalize.fixpoint 233 / 2.2 k; ops.call 126 / 2.7 k; normalizer.decode 22 / 56; rest <= 11; per turn p50 22.7 k, p99 630 k, max 5.18 M (limit 10 M). Rules mode is about half of these.
- Evals before -> after (rebased on c3909ca, identical numbers): rules operational 1239/1296 right 95.6 %, 0.5 % wrong; long 56.5 %, 22.7 % wrong; operational-ro 82.1 %, 2.5 % wrong; dialogues 90.1 % dialogues, 97 % turns. encoder+n1 operational 98.6 %, 0.3 % wrong; long 94.2 %, 1.9 % wrong; operational-ro 85.8 %, 2.6 % wrong; dialogues 89.5 %, 96.8 % turns.
- Overhead: 15 ns per tick, 8 k ticks per rules turn (22 k encoder) = 0.1 to 0.35 ms against p50 15 to 22 ms (about 1 to 2 %).
- Gates: `npm test` (PUPPETEER_CORE) 1176 tests, 1174 pass, 0 fail, 0 cancelled, 2 skipped; webchat rebuilt. No world files touched except ops.mjs comments (no behaviour change).
## COMPUTE-BUDGET A (2026-11-10, worker): engine worker, watchdog that terminates, no repetition, evidence, fault injection (items 1, 3, 5, 6)
- **Engine off the page thread.** `docs/webchat/ui/engine-worker.mjs` (DialogSession, formalizers, rules translator, interpreter, world operations, dispatcher/normalizer runtimes) and `docs/webchat/ui/engine-client.mjs` (page side: the `session` proxy, watchdog, bad-input memory, ORT bridge). `app.mjs` keeps UI, speech, effects, storage. The ORT workers stay owned by the page: the engine worker asks for one per lane over a `MessageChannel` (`__psys2OrtSpawn` in `ui/ort-host.mjs`), so killing the engine worker stops them too. Wires carry `sk` (short keys), the legends stay in the worker; the debug view loads a second, uncompiled engine on the page only in developer mode. Build: the page no longer preloads `lib/dialog` or the world operations.
- **Watchdog.** Abort message, 300 ms grace, then `terminate()` and a new worker from the last committed world state; answer "That took too long, I stopped it."; stage (the worker posts every `guard` stage change, even from inside a loop), input and tab go to the console, the phrase log (`outcome: timeout`, `stage`) and `engineClient.stats.kills`. Stop uses the same path (Stop on a loop: 84-95 ms on screen). A new request waits for an abandoned one to settle, so the next message never lands in a stuck worker.
- **No repetition.** Inputs (normalized text + tab) that needed a kill, ended in `BudgetExceeded` or crashed the worker are remembered for the session: the next identical message gets the stored outcome, dry runs get `known-slow`, a worker restart does not rerun them, the client never re-sends. `tests/formalize-calls.test.mjs`: `_formalize` at most 4 calls per message (27 messages, en and ro).
- **Evidence.** `PerformanceObserver('longtask')` (over 200 ms) with the host stage and engine stage into the phrase-log record (`longtasks`), `window.psys2.longTasks`, and the export's new top-level `diagnostics` (long tasks, engine starts/crashes/kills/refusals, ORT counters); `reply.budget` (ticks per site) is stored in the record when the step budget exists (feature-detected: `lib/dialog/budget.mjs` is passed `{ budget }` to `session.send` and `dryRead` once agent B lands it). Debug view: Models > "Engine worker", Timing > "Step budget".
- **Fault injection.** `?psys2test=1` + `window.__psys2Fault = { text: 'op' | 'matcher' | 'translator' }` makes the worker spin in a world operation, the rules matcher or the translator for that exact text (inert otherwise). `scripts/test-webchat-budget.mjs` / `tests/webchat-budget.test.mjs`: per fault echo 3-4 ms (limit 100), largest heartbeat gap 69-80 ms, watchdog ends the turn, worker replaced once, next message works (0.5-0.8 s), same input / upper case / dry run not run again; Stop during a loop; hands-free voice (the dry run is the request that gets killed, the typed repeat is then refused); evidence. 29/29 checks at 4x.
- **Main-thread cost (4x, rules, headless, noisy because the machine is shared).** Long-task sum per turn before -> after: "fă o casă verde și una galbenă" 1950 -> 854 ms (max 805 -> 239), "I have two kids" 1864 -> 552 (max 1347 -> 107), "my favourite color is green" 297 -> 0..53, "desenează un soare" 715 -> 522. CPU profile of the two heavy turns after: JS self time on the page about 40 ms; the rest is the browser's own rendering of the drawing under 4x throttle. Typed soak (`--minutes 4 --typed --profile --rules`): 10/10, 74 turns, mean 103 ms, max 304 ms, input echo median 20 ms, main thread 25 % busy, 0 watchdog endings. The engine time per turn (about 90 ms mean, worker) is unchanged by this task; item 7 (per-turn cost) is agent C's.
- **Known limits.** After a worker restart the discourse memory is not carried over (world states and the message counter are). The browser Translation API is window-only: other languages use the rules translator in the worker. Modules the worker imports use plain URLs (no import map in workers): a stale library file from the HTTP cache can pair with a fresh worker for up to the host's cache lifetime; the worker, ORT worker, worlds index and world operations are versioned.
- Counts: `npm test` with PUPPETEER_CORE: see the commit message. `npm run test:browser`: all green after updating `test-webchat-nx` (the ORT client is a mirror now; `restart` goes through the worker) and `tests/perf-round2.test.mjs` (no preload of the engine).
## COMPUTE-BUDGET D (2026-11-10, worker): world helpers ticked, discourse memory across a restart, Romanian bridge, versioned worker imports
- **Probable cause of the user's freeze found (item 3 investigation).** `checkTranslation` in `docs/webchat/ui/translation.mjs` called `names(src)`, whose regex `/(^|[.!?]\s+)|(\p{Lu}\p{L}+)/gu` has an alternative that matches the EMPTY string at index 0: `exec()` returned the same empty match for ever (`lastIndex` stays 0), a synchronous infinite loop. It ran after EVERY successful browser (on-device Translator) translation, i.e. on every Romanian message in a Chrome that has the Translation API ready, whatever the Reading strategy (Rules only does not touch it); the user types and speaks Romanian ("fă o casă verde și una galbenă"), the reviewer's headless Chrome has no on-device model, so the browser path never ran there. The code is from the first webchat commit (b900f22); `tests/webchat-detect.test.mjs` never reached it (its fake translators fail an earlier check or never return). Fixed (non-empty regex), regression test `tests/translation-bridge.test.mjs` runs `checkTranslation` in a worker thread with a 5 s kill. Compute-budget B's comment "regex exec with the g flag advances lastIndex each pass" was false for this loop; I checked the two other `exec` loops (`src/translate/normalize.mjs` TOKEN_RE, `ui/trace.mjs`, both non-empty by construction) and rewrote their comments to say why. Since the engine moved into the worker (A) the browser translator was not used at all, so a build with the worker would not have frozen from this; the bridge (below) brings the browser translator back, with the fix.
- **1. World helpers without a ctx.** The turn budget is reachable from any world file through `globalThis[Symbol.for('psys2.budget')]` (the shared handle, set when `src/dialog/budget.mjs` loads, a no-op outside a turn); each world file declares `const budget = { tick: (site, n = 1) => globalThis[Symbol.for('psys2.budget')]?.tick(site, n) }` (worlds cannot import from `src`; the build copies a world as plain files). New ticks: art.draw `crowd` (every placement candidate: `art.place`, n = objects), `parseNP` and `resolveNP` (`art.np`, were two `// bounded` comments on recursion), diagram.sketch `placeFree` grid (`diagram.place`, 625 spots x (shapes + arrow points)), `routeEdges` clearance (`diagram.route`), id counters (`diagram.name`, two former comments), kids.time fact chain BFS (`time.chain`, former comment), media.music `stackUp` (`music.stack`, former comment), docs.reader id counter (`reader.id`, former comment), clause/NP/PP loops and the union-find `find` of the analyser (`reader.np`, `reader.pp`, `reader.merge`). The diagram ENGINE (layout / untangle / datalog solver / path search; 5000 lines, converted and never scanned before) gets 17 ticks through `scripts/convert-diagram-engine.mjs` (`BUDGET_PATCHES`, anchored string replacements, each must match exactly once; the generated files are still reproducible): `diagram.layout` (separation pass n^2, force layout n^2 per iteration, crossing reduction, toposort/BFS/DFS worklists, template fill), `diagram.solve` (every `solve` call and fixpoint iteration), `diagram.path`, `diagram.name`, `diagram.words`. `tests/loop-guard.test.mjs` now scans EVERY world module (`worlds/**/*.mjs` minus `datagen.mjs`, `vendor/`, the generated engine): that found 78 unguarded loops in 9 files nobody scanned (analyze.mjs 15, logic.mjs engine 20, auto-layout 18, templates 9, text.mjs 6, abc.mjs 4, understand.mjs 3, model.mjs 2, target.mjs 1); the engine ones are covered by the ticks above and a test that counts them, the other 27 linear scans got truthful `// bounded:` reasons (index only moves one way over a finite list, `hops` capped at 4, `count` capped at 9, Euclid, one dead `&& false` branch). Review of the 200 `// bounded:` comments of B in src, worlds and ui: **9 converted** (parseNP, resolveNP, 2 diagram counters, kids.time BFS, music stackUp, docs.reader id, and the two `lon` while loops of `ui/tabs/map/geo.mjs`, which looped for ever on an infinite longitude and are now closed-form); **12 new ticks** where there was no comment at all (crowd, placeFree, routeEdges, 4 analyser sites, ...) plus the 17 engine ticks; the rest are linear passes over a finite list or text, fixed-size candidate lists or load-time graph walks with a seen set, and stay comments. New limits in `budget-limits.mjs` (measured over operational / long / ro / dialogues in rules mode: `art.place` max 1.5 k, `art.np` 72, `diagram.*` max 42, `music.stack` 3; limits 20 k to 2 M). `tests/world-budget.test.mjs`: the handle, a drawing placement out of budget ends as too complex and leaves the picture as it was, the diagram layout is ticked and stops.
- **2. Discourse memory across a restart.** `Discourse.toJSON()/load()/fromJSON()` (plain JSON; a pending repair is re-linked to the very action and frame it names), `DialogSession.memoryJSON()` (discourse + the ASTRA snapshot of `astra/persist.mjs` + recent worlds + last game) and `restoreMemory()`; the worker returns it in `meta.memory` with every turn and restores it from `restoreMeta`. `tests/discourse-memory.test.mjs` (JSON round trip and structuredClone are lossless, ellipsis "And from Berlin?" resolves after a restart, "undo that" and a pending "Draw" -> "a cat" survive, a bad record is refused); browser scenario `memory` in `scripts/test-webchat-budget.mjs` (a watchdog kill, then "And from Berlin?" still answers "Paris and Berlin ..."). Size of the per-turn record after 4 turns is under 400 KB (asserted), usually a few KB.
- **3. Romanian in the worker.** Before the worker the page used the browser Translator and LanguageDetector on the critical path (strategy auto, 400 ms per call, then the rules; HANG3 only bounded it). In the worker `self.Translator` does not exist, so every non-English message used the rules. Headless Chrome has no on-device model, so the browser path equals the rules path there: operational-ro in the (rules) browser path = the rules eval, 82.1 % right / 2.5 % wrong, `scripts/test-webchat-ro-rp5.mjs` 43/43; a real Chrome with the model can differ, and that is not measurable here. So the bridge: `createBrowserBackend()` (page: the HANG3 machinery) and `createRemoteBackend()` (worker: `{type:'bt'}` to the page, `{type:'bt-res'}` back, 700 ms deadline, then the rules), `createSwitchTranslator({ backend })`; the checker still runs in the worker. Browser scenario `bridge` with fake Translator/LanguageDetector on the window: ok (usage `browser-api` for detection and translation, 911 ms) and hang (no answer: the rules translated, "browser translation timed out after 400 ms" in the trace, 1.7 s, no kill, no errors). Unit tests `tests/translation-bridge.test.mjs` (answer, deadline, failure, checker failure, no API: no message sent).
- **4. Versioned worker imports.** `scripts/build-webchat.mjs versionGraph`: every module of `lib/` and `worlds/` gets a Merkle version (own text + everything it imports; an import cycle is one unit) and every relative specifier in those generated files becomes `<specifier>?v=<target version>`, including literal `import('./x.mjs')`; the page's import map and `window.__psys2Mod` carry the same versions for them (one instance), `ui/` and `app.mjs` keep content hashes. The worker imports `lib/` and `ui/` through `globalThis.__psys2Url` (versions passed at init); `translation.mjs` has its own `withDeadline` (it imported `lib/formalizer/guard.mjs`), the dispatcher / normalizer hosts load `ort-host.mjs` by versioned URL and `ort-host.mjs` its `lib` imports; the worker's ui files have no relative import (tested). A changed leaf changes only the versions of its dependents (unit test with a cycle); unchanged subgraphs keep their URL. `tests/build-versioning.test.mjs` (3 tests, 194 imports of the build checked against the version map and the import map) and `tests/webchat-freeze.test.mjs` (the server logs every request with its query: no `lib/`, `worlds/` or `ui/` module request without `?v=`, from the page or the worker, after the new build is served over a profile cached from 1e667bb). Note: all generated files under `docs/webchat/lib` and `worlds` change in this commit (specifiers), expect a rebuild conflict at merge: run `node scripts/build-webchat.mjs` again.
- **Gates.** Rules evals unchanged: operational 1296, right 95.6 %, wrong 0.5 %; long 154, 56.5 % / 22.7 %; operational-ro 2480, 82.1 % / 2.5 %; dialogues 470 (97 % of turns, 90.2 % of dialogues). `npm test` with PUPPETEER_CORE: 1194 tests, 1192 pass, 0 fail, 0 cancelled, 2 skipped. `npm run test:browser`: 7 of 8 files first run; the failing one was the new unversioned-module assertion counting the OLD build's requests (the harness log was not reset at the switch); fixed, `webchat-freeze` passes. test-world art.draw 562/562, diagram.sketch 756/756, docs.reader 209/209, kids.time 217/217, media.music 401/401. `node scripts/loop-violations.mjs`: 0 unguarded (was 78 unscanned).
- **Not done / notes.** The encoder-mode evals were not rerun (nothing in the reading path changed; the rules ones are identical). The Merkle graph changes lib/worlds URLs more often than per-file hashes did when a low-level file (budget.mjs, guard.mjs) changes: every dependent re-downloads; unchanged subgraphs stay cached.

## COMPUTE-BUDGET E (2026-11-10, worker): restart clock, start limit, main-thread cost per turn
- **1. Restart defect.** The replacement worker was already started at kill time (`respawn()`); the defect was that the turn clock could start while it was still starting or while an abandoned request was still being ended (the old check only looked at `booting` at one instant). New `engineClient.settled()` / `restarting`: resolves when a worker is ready and no abandoned request or pending restore is left; `process()` shows "restarting…" in the thinking bubble, waits (later messages queue behind the turn as before), and starts the watchdog, backstop and "thinking" clock only after that. Stop during "restarting…" ends the turn as stopped. New limit `LIMITS.startMs` (Settings › Developer › Time limits, default 60 s, 10-300 s): a worker not ready in time is terminated once and the engine stays down (`stats.startTimeouts`, event `engine-down`); the waiting message ends with "The engine could not restart. Please reload the page." (`data-engine-down`), `detail.engineDown` in the phrase log, an `engineDown` debug record, console warning. No chain of kills. New scenario `restart` in `scripts/test-webchat-budget.mjs` (message right behind Stop gets a real answer; a worker that never starts = distinct error, one start timeout, logged; test hook `window.__psys2StartHang`). `--only restart,stop` 10/10 at throttle 6 and 1.
- **2. Main-thread cost.** New `scripts/perf-turn-cost.mjs` (CDP `TaskDuration` per message, 4x, incl. an idle reference window). Causes found with CDP tracing and idle A/B runs: (a) the avatar's DiceBear SVG animates inner nodes: ~200 ms of main-thread time per 3 s of idle at 4x (the largest single cost; a paused animation costs almost as much, so `animation: none` is used) -> `.p2-rest` on the root during a turn and 3 s after; (b) the draw tab canvas was a fixed 2000x1400 redrawn and committed every frame -> backing store follows the displayed size x dpr (cap 2000), ResizeObserver, hidden tab paints nothing; (c) `.p2-robot` float animation repainted the page (~150 ms per 3 s) -> `contain: layout paint; will-change: transform`. Not causes (measured): the message list (no forced layout from the app per turn except 2 scrollHeight reads), style recalc (~20 ms per turn), the busy veil toggles, trace panels.
- **Numbers, total TaskDuration per turn at 4x (typing of the message by the harness included, about 100 ms; before -> after):** "what is 12 plus 30" 186 -> ~190 (target < 300 met); "desenează un soare" 511 -> ~330; "fă o casă verde și una galbenă" 926 -> ~670-890 (noisy, target < 600 NOT met); "I have two kids" 256 -> ~250. The remaining cost of the house turn is about 70 animation frames of compositor commits (Commit 250 ms: the main thread waits for the software rasterizer of the headless 2-vCPU host) plus ~90 ms script; not reduced further.
- **Gates.** `npm test` 1194 tests, 0 fail, 0 cancelled; `npm run test:browser` 8/8; budget scenarios at throttle 1 and 6: budget scenarios 61/61 at throttle 1 and at throttle 6; rebuilt with `node scripts/build-webchat.mjs`.

## COMPUTE-BUDGET F (2026-11-10, worker): the loop guard refuses unsafe regex loops, `// bounded:` comments audited away, fake on-device Translator in the browser harness
- **1. Regex loops.** `scripts/lib/loop-scan.mjs` now checks every `X.exec(` inside a while, do or for loop (also `for (;;)` and custom for). Refused unless (a) the regex provably cannot match the empty string (`minLen`: a small regex parser, every top-level alternative must consume at least one character; lookarounds, anchors, `\b` and back-references count 0, anything it cannot prove fails; the old `names()` regex `(^|[.!?]\s+)|(\p{Lu}\p{L}+)` gives 0), or (b) the body advances `lastIndex` (`++`, `+=`, `= ... + 1`). A tick or a comment does not excuse it. A non-global regex in a while condition is refused (endless), a regex not resolvable to a literal in the file (`new RegExp`) is refused, `matchAll` / `replaceAll` with a non-global regex is refused (they throw). The regex is resolved to the closest definition above the use. To read flags the scanner's `strip()` now keeps regex flags (it blanked them before). Converted to `matchAll`: `src/translate/normalize.mjs` tokenizer, `docs/webchat/ui/trace.mjs` (colorSop), `docs/webchat/ui/translation.mjs` (`names`), and the diagram engine's `templateOf` (`scripts/convert-diagram-engine.mjs` patch + `logic.mjs`). A new test checks the regex rules over `src`, `worlds` (engine included) and `docs/webchat/ui`. The old trigger is a unit row ("fă o casă verde" shape: the old regex fails the guard).
- **2. `// bounded:` audit.** Comments before: 145 in the scanned files (src 76, worlds 53, docs/webchat/ui 19, the rest prose). After: 0, and `tests/loop-guard.test.mjs` asserts that none is left; a bounded comment excuses nothing now (`scan(text, { strict: false })` only for tooling; `node scripts/audit-bounded.mjs [--list]` lists leftovers).
  - **Converted to ticks:** about 123 comments, 124 tick sites (src 71, worlds 53). The sites are named `<file>.<function>`; worlds use the global-handle shim line. Euclid recursions (`kids.arith`, `media.music`) became ticked loops. `scripts/measure-ticks.mjs` (7497 turns, rules) shows the largest new sites at p99 73 k (`index.run`), 43 k (`index.readString`), far under the default 1 M per site; no limit entries added.
  - **Removed because statically verifiable (no tick, no comment):** 19 in `docs/webchat/ui`: 5 recursions whose self-call sits in a deferred callback (setTimeout, addEventListener, on... handlers, `.then`: feedback, documents, profile, robot, speech; the scanner now understands those), 5 loops that `await` every pass (the two HTTP body readers, ort-worker drain, the two idle waits of engine-worker), 1 depth-capped walk (`annotateWires`, the scanner accepts `depth > N` with `depth + 1` in every self-call), 3 in debug.mjs (ring trimmed with one `splice`; `diffPaths` and `jsonTree` given a real depth cap), a counting loop in diagram.mjs, one in engine-client, the deliberate fault-injection `spin()` (marked `loop-guard-allow`, listed in the test with a count of exactly 1). In src: `matcher.matchWords` became a counting `for` of at most `words.length` passes (exact: each pass advances k by 1, the OP_WORDS phrases are not empty), a for-of over `set.list()`, and the exec loop above.
  - **Kept with a test:** none (the one allowed infinite loop is asserted by count).
  - **False or misleading claims found:** the `names()` exec comment (the freeze); `jsonTree` "with a depth cap" had no cap (added one); documents.mjs `render()` was a shadowed parameter, not recursion (renamed); the tight `matchWords` loop could not take a tick: the first attempt (a tick per pass) cost 22 M ticks in one turn, 1.9 G over the sets, and tripped the 1 M site limit on "what is seven tymes six pls", so it was restructured instead. Parsing rule changes: `tests/loop-guard.test.mjs` gained ~30 scanner rows (comment excuses nothing, await, deferred callbacks, depth cap, regex rows, minLen table).
- **3. Fake on-device APIs in the harness.** `scripts/lib/fake-browser-apis.mjs`, installed by default in `openChat` (so by every browser script and `npm run test:browser`; `openChat({ browserApis: false })` leaves the page without them). Page side: `window.Translator` / `window.LanguageDetector` (this Chrome has native ones, so the fake is installed with `defineProperty`; the older `window.ai.translator` / `window.ai.languageDetector` shape with `shape: 'ai'`, which hides the native ones). Modes: `success` (the English of the rules translator, run in node and exposed through `exposeFunction`, and the rules detection; ro to en only, en to ro reports unavailable), `slow` (1500 ms, past the 700 ms bridge deadline), `hang`, `wrong` with `empty | garbage | numbers | names | negation | punct`; `chat.fake.set({ mode })` flips it, `chat.fake.calls()` lists the page calls. New scenario `node scripts/test-webchat-budget.mjs --only ondevice` (41 checks) plus `bridge` (now success/hang through the same fake): the first row is the exact old trigger "fă o casă verde și una galbenă" (checker ran and accepted, detector of the page used, both houses drawn); wrong outputs are rejected with the right reason (empty translation, question form, numbers, names, negation) and the rules answer; punctuation-first runs to the end; slow falls back to the rules with "timed out"; the older ai shape works; every turn checks echo < 100 ms, heartbeat gap < 500 ms, no kill. Verified that it catches the bug: with the old regex put back temporarily the first row fails ("That took too long, I stopped it", worker kill).
- **Gates.** `npm test` (PUPPETEER_CORE): 1198 tests, 1196 pass, 0 fail, 0 cancelled, 2 skipped. `npm run test:browser` 8/8. `test-webchat-budget.mjs --only stop,restart --throttle 6` 10/10 (stop-then-next-message already fixed in 86772e8). test-world art.draw 562, data.charts 267, diagram.sketch 756, docs.editor 221, docs.reader 209, games.chess 257, games.tictactoe 160, kids.arith 217, media.music 401, ui.avatar 327, ui.help 83, user.profile 126: all pass. Rules evals unchanged: operational 1296 / right 1239 (95.6 %) / wrong 6 (0.5 %); long 154 / 87 / 35 wrong (56.5 % / 22.7 %); operational-ro 2480 / 2035 (82.1 %) / 61 wrong (2.5 %); dialogues 470, 424 dialogues right (90.2 %), 2174 of 2241 turns (97.0 %). `node scripts/loop-violations.mjs`: 0. Webchat rebuilt. Spec 06 updated (static enforcement, regex loops, hot loops, fake translator).
- **Not done / notes.** The scanner resolves a regex only when it is a literal in the same file; anything else must use `matchAll`. An `await` in a loop body is accepted as yielding (an endless async loop would still burn CPU, but it cannot freeze the thread). The `loop-guard-allow` marker is honoured only for the one file/count listed in the test.

## LANG-FIXED (2026-11-10, worker): the selected language is the only language (items 1-8)
- **1-2. One source of truth.** `settings.language` (en | ro) only. `sessionLang`, `noteSessionLang`, `reply.switchedLanguage`, the strong-evidence overrides of an explicit language in `DialogSession._send` (strongEn read as English under Română, strongRo read as Romanian under English) and the `ui.shell` language branch of `applyShell` are gone. With Română selected every message goes ro→en and every reply comes back in Romanian; English words ("ok", "stop", "play little star") are handled by the Romanian pipeline. The engine's `auto` stays for the CLI/server only (RD5 behaviour kept there). Only the selector, Settings > Language or an explicit command ("vorbește în engleză", "switch to Romanian", "speak English": the existing `ui.language` operation, confirmation "Bine, am trecut la engleză.") changes it; the host takes the command from the turn's reply state, only when the selector did not move meanwhile. "treci pe engleză" is not read by the shell patterns (noted, not changed).
- **3. No auto.** Settings version 3: a saved `auto` (or anything else) becomes the browser language (`ro*` → ro, otherwise en) and is saved; `speechLang` is dropped (a saved `en-GB` becomes `englishVariant`). Voice keeps the voice, the rate and, with English selected, the English variant (en-US / en-GB).
- **4. Detector off the turn path.** `LanguageDetector`/rules detector are used only by the engine worker's new `hint` request, sent after a TYPED reply (never voice, never knownBad): if rules evidence is strong, the detector agrees, >= 3 words and 8 letters, a one-line hint under the message ("Pare engleză — schimb limba?" + button "Treci pe engleză"). Never automatic.
- **5. One module.** `docs/webchat/ui/language.mjs`: `createLanguage` (`get`, `set(lang, reason)`, `onChange`, `tag`, English variant), `LANG_TAGS`, `normalize`, `browserLanguage`, `hintText`, `suggestSwitch`. Used by app.mjs, speech.mjs (tags), voice-noise.mjs (the dry run reads the selected language only, no second try in the other one), translation.mjs (language list, passed by the worker; no static import there, see its header), the engine client (`getLanguage`) and the worker (`normalize`, `hint`). **Lines removed:** about 62 (app.mjs 25: `sessionLang`, `noteSessionLang`, the speechLang auto/follow branch, the shell language branch, the switchedLanguage handling; dialog/index.mjs 11 of evidence overrides; voice-noise 8; speech/translation/settings the rest); added: language.mjs 70 + about 100 in the hosts (hint, voice note, migration).
- **6. Phrase log.** Every record has `selectedLang`; voice records have `recLang` (the tag the recognizer really started with, `speech.recLang()`), also on ignored voice records and after `completeRecord`.
- **7. Tests.** `tests/language.test.mjs` (unit: language.mjs, migration, hint rules, engine never overrides, switch commands, 8 tests); `scripts/test-webchat-language.mjs` + `tests/webchat-language.test.mjs` (browser, fake recognizer, spy LanguageDetector): ro30 = 30 voice turns with English-looking finals + English typed message + app tab switch + hidden/visible tab + worker kill/restart + page reload, every SpeechRecognition start ro-RO (18+ starts), selector stays Română, no LanguageDetector call in voice turns, recLang on every record; en10 symmetric (en-US); command (hint button, typed/spoken switch commands, selector, saved); voice; replay09e (the 21 records of expectations-2026-11-09e replayed as voice, recLang asserted). The phone-export `replay` scenario also asserts ro-RO and recLang. The old auto-following `lang` voice scenario was removed (behaviour deleted). Old tests that relied on evidence-switching were rewritten (ro-sessions, translate-session; test-webchat-ui feedback row uses Română; freeze-profile selects Română for its Romanian lines; budget translator fault case runs with Română selected because an English session no longer calls the translator).
- **8. Reply voice.** `pickVoice` uses the chosen voice only when it speaks the selected language, else the first voice of the tag; with none the utterance still carries ro-RO and the default voice speaks. Settings > Voice says "No Romanian voice is installed on this device: answers are read with the default voice" or "Reads Romanian with: <name>".
- **Gates.** `npm test` 1202 tests, 0 fail, 0 cancelled (2 skipped as before); `npm run test:browser` 10/10; `node scripts/test-webchat-language.mjs` 32/32 at throttle 1 and at throttle 6 (the first 6x run exposed a race in my test: a click while the recognizer was resuming after a hidden tab; fixed in the test); `scripts/test-webchat-voice.mjs --throttle 6 --only replay` 10/10; `scripts/test-webchat-ro-rp5.mjs` 43/43; build rebuilt. Operational-ro (rules mode, language auto): 82.1% right, 2.5% wrong, 2178 Romanian / 299 English / 3 mixed replies, identical to the baseline of the parent commit. Spec 06 (language, hint, recognition language, settings tabs), spec 05 (language selection) and ADR-002 (superseded bullets) updated.

Of 15 764 code lines in the 22 `ops.mjs` and helper modules: state and rules 2 071 (13%), queries and aggregates 2 912 (18%), text handling 1 017 (6%), engine/geometry/search 5 844 (37%; diagram.sketch's engine is 4 733), reply wording 1 166 (7%), tables/glue 2 754 (17%): **32% replaceable**. Above 50% replaceable: kids.arith 92, kids.shop 82, kids.tasks 78, docs.editor 67, docs.reader 63, games.guess 62, geo.map 58, games.hangman 57, kids.shapes 57, data.charts 52. Engine-heavy, keep their engines and move state and validation: diagram.sketch 6, art.draw 13, games.tictactoe 27, games.chess 38. The classification is by function name and body, corrected by hand (`OVERRIDES` in the script); counts are per function, glue = lines outside functions.
- **Workarounds, as issues for the library session (the vendored file is untouched).**
  1. One function registry per World, shared with its builtins (`add`, `sub`, `count`...): the tasks actions `add`/`count` clobbered the builtins used by effects and calcs. Worked around by prefixing every registered name (`tasks.add`, `shop.revise`). Wanted: action functions in their own namespace.
  2. `query` returns no derived answer-wire attributes: `from`/`wires` are empty for supposed answers, so wording facts (basket text, cost of a refused change, the leader's count) need second queries from `voice`. Wanted: the supposed answer wire (keys set by `core.on`) in the result.
  3. `refused` lists `wires` that no longer exist once the supposition is undone, and a deny's `why` is one fixed string with no values.
  4. No `check` of a whole state: the audit tells the wires to a fresh World and counts refusals. Wanted `world.check()` with the violations. Also, a purchase that leaves the money negative is accepted by `tell` and refused only when the balance is asked (a deny on a supposed question wire is not a deny on the state); a `core.deny` over the fluent would make `tell` refuse it.
  5. `fill` of a key that was told (the user said count=1) is a `conflict`; withdrawal works for defaulted values only. "three of them" needed `replace` of the whole wire rebuilt from `attrs` text. Wanted `world.revise(id, key, value)`.
  6. A told wire whose key a rule later filled shows the filled value in `attrs` (`holding count=6` after "has 14", "eats 8"): re-telling held wires is not idempotent (my first audit gave 26 false conflicts). `meta.stated` has the original. Wanted a stable export of the told wires as told.
  7. `abduce` needs the told wire without the goal key and the goal as a pattern; `query` with `seek=paid` is `missing`. Wanted abduction through `query`.
  8. Still open from the pilot: G14 "received N" with an unknown start, G10 ties, impossible events refused when told with no way to report them (recorded in `session.rs.refused`, the user is not told). The words of replies (a/an, plurals, "cost/costs") stay in `voice`.
  9. The library's parser accepts `#` comments, ours does not: `matrix.sop` has comments, the files our parser reads (`patterns.sop`, `templates.sop`, `operations.sop`) must not.
  10. Division by zero and the "about 3.33" rounding of a non-terminating quotient stay in `kids.arith/fns.mjs` `voice` (two edge conventions of the old operation): G11/G13.
- **Not done / limits.** The operations' per-world state is not kept for kids.shop and kids.arith in matrix mode (tasks has `toState`: lists, for `referents`/`summary` and the dialogue layer's snapshots); the dialogue layer's undo offers restore the ops state, not the library (3 dialogue turns above). "undo that" takes back the last told turn of the last six; undoing after "three of them" removes the purchase. A statement the library refuses is acknowledged "OK" to the user. The matrix-only patterns ("N of them", "I got N lei back for a book", "what did I pay") are narrow examples, no shapes/phrases yet. A changed Reasoner setting loses the library facts (the worker restarts from the state snapshot).

## BUDGET-HEARTBEAT: the 550 ms heartbeat gap in the on-device scenario

- **Cause (product code).** Not the slow translator (the page is idle while it is awaited; the bridge and rules translation run in the engine worker). The gap was the Music tab's gesture hook (`docs/webchat/ui/tabs/music.mjs`): the first `keydown`/`pointerdown` anywhere on the page called `engine.unlock()`, which loads Tone.js and builds its Reverb (a synchronous noise-buffer fill, `set buffer` in `vendor/music/tone.mjs`). That landed on the first key of the first typed message of every fresh page, i.e. inside the probe window, at 0.3-0.6 s of main thread on a slow CPU. Evidence: CDP Profiler at throttle 6 on 2 pinned vCPUs (`taskset -c 0,1`, which reproduced 525-670 ms gaps in the first turn of each fresh page, including `slow`): `set buffer`/`white`/`o` in `music/tone.mjs` ~330 ms self time, gaps 300/279/899 ms around the first key; after the fix the profile shows only `rough.mjs`/`avatar.mjs` (a few ms each) and no `tone.mjs`.
- **Fix.** The gesture hook now acts only while the Music tab is on screen (`holder.offsetParent !== null`); `play()` still calls `unlock()` itself (sticky user activation is enough for the browser's autoplay policy). Nothing of Tone is loaded for a chat message.
- **Harness fixes found on the way.** (1) `openRo` in `scripts/test-webchat-budget.mjs` still used `language: 'auto'` (removed by LANG-FIXED), so the `bridge` and `ondevice` scenarios never reached the translator (10+ FAILs, none with a freeze); now `language: 'ro'`, and the two detector assertions now state that the page detector is not consulted for a fixed Romanian session. (2) `scripts/test-webchat-voice.mjs` (stayon): read the mic state right after the recognizer started while it is still `starting` until the fake's onstart timer; the faster page exposed the race, now waits for `listening`.
- **Not a fake problem.** The fake's `slow` mode is an async `setTimeout` on the page and does no synchronous work (the rules run in node via `exposeFunction`).
- **Residual.** Under `taskset -c 0,1` at throttle 6 the non-slow `wrong output (empty|names)` turns still sometimes show 300-800 ms (JS profile shows only ~100 ms; the rest is `(program)`/CPU starvation of the 2 cores shared by renderer, workers and GPU). Unpinned runs: max gap 261 ms at throttle 4, 409 ms at 6; `slow` 41/107/118 ms at throttle 1/4/6.
- **Gates:** `ondevice` passes at throttle 1, 4, 6; `npm test` 1221 pass / 0 fail / 0 cancelled; `npm run test:browser` 11/11.

I don't know how many Ana had before."), `ambiguous`, `soft=true` + `issues()` ("Lily only had 3 cards, so Lily cannot give away 5."); (10) division by zero: the library says `unsupported` with `why`, the words stay; the 2-decimal rounding is `core.precision decimals=2`. kids.arith 217/217 (was 210/217), kids.shop 147/147, kids.tasks 232/232 with the switch on; unchanged with it off.
- **Remain, and why:** (9) the library's parser accepts `#` comments, ours does not: `patterns.sop`, `templates.sop`, `operations.sop` cannot have them (our parser); reply wording stays in `voice` (a/an, plurals, costs/cost, "about", "from Tom", the zero-divisor sentence); which item to name when a price is missing is a lookup of the price wires; kids.tasks' text handling (split "milk, eggs and bread", list names, matching a named thing) is not a matrix job and stays in `fns.mjs`; `gotBack` tells a wire from a function.
